<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which named protein complex is the most specific complex-level cellular component associated with this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> COP9 signalosome<br><br><strong>B.</strong> SCF ubiquitin ligase complex<br><br><strong>C.</strong> 26S proteasome<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Cul3-RING ubiquitin ligase complex<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>To determine the associated protein complex, I analyzed the protein sequence for characteristic domain features:<br><br>**Domain Analysis:**<br>- **N-terminal region:** The sequence from approximately residue 10-130 contains hallmarks of a **BTB (Broad-Complex, Tramtrack, Bric-a-brac) domain**, characterized by the hydrophobic core pattern and the conserved motif &quot;VGGQLFQADRALLVEHCGFFRGLFRSGMRETRAAEVRLGVLSAGGFRATLQVLR...&quot;<br>- **C-terminal region:** The sequence from approximately residue 190 onward contains repeated motifs starting with &quot;LYIVGG&quot; (e.g., &quot;NKLYIVGGVRGASKEVVELGFCYDPDGG...&quot;), which are characteristic of **Kelch repeats**.<br><br>**Functional Interpretation:**<br>The **BTB-Kelch** domain architecture is the signature of substrate receptors that function within the **Cul3-RING ubiquitin ligase complex**. The BTB domain mediates direct binding to Cul3, while the Kelch repeats serve as substrate-recognition modules. This is distinct from SCF complexes, which use F-box proteins as substrate adapters (binding via Skp1/Cul1), not BTB proteins.<br><br>**Evaluation of choices:**<br>- **A. COP9 signalosome:** Regulates cullin-RING ligases but is not where BTB-Kelch proteins reside.<br>- **B. SCF ubiquitin ligase complex:** Uses F-box proteins (via Skp1), not BTB proteins.<br>- **C. 26S proteasome:** A proteolytic complex, unrelated to E3 ligase substrate receptors.<br>- **D. Cul3-RING ubiquitin ligase complex:** BTB-Kelch proteins are the canonical substrate receptors for this complex. ✓<br><br>2. answer: D</td>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> For the protein represented by the supplied amino-acid sequence, which named complex is its most specific supported complex-level cellular-component association? In mature 20S proteasome beta subunits, the catalytic threonine is preceded by this conserved motif (e.g., human PSMB5: &quot;TTLYIIGGG&quot;; yeast Pre2: &quot;TTTLYIVGG&quot;). This is NOT a generic sequence feature — it is a signature of the threonine protease active site unique to proteasome beta subunits.<br><br>**Additional structural context:** The overall fold is consistent with a proteasome subunit architecture, including the characteristic β-grasp/proteasome subunit fold. The presence of a long N-terminal region likely represents an extended propeptide (which is cleaved to expose the catalytic threonine, as is the case for all 20S beta subunits).<br><br>**The &quot;GGTWRLL&quot; motifs** in the C-terminal region could superficially suggest AAA+ ATPase domains (found in 19S Rpt subunits), but as the question warns, we should not infer complex membership from generic features. The &quot;EHEC&quot; found is insufficiently specific.<br>- SCF ubiquitin ligase complex: No F-box, SKP1, or cullin signatures present.<br>- Cul3-RING ligase: No BTB, cullin repeat, or RING finger domains.<br><br>**Conclusion:** The &quot;GNKLYIVGG&quot; proteasome beta subunit-specific catalytic motif is the strongest, most specific evidence. The 20S core particle is a defining component of the 26S proteasome complex.<br><br>2. answer: C</td>
